His engineering skills and intellect had served him well during his adventures, and he had even managed to level up his abilities, growing stronger and smarter each day.\nOne fateful afternoon, as he delved deep into the forests of the planetoid, Tails stumbled upon a mysterious chamber hidden beneath the roots of an ancient tree. The entrance, though well-hidden, bore the signs of past explorations. Piqued by curiosity, Tails ventured inside.\nThe chamber was dimly lit by the flickering light of magical torches, revealing dusty shelves filled with scrolls and documents that had been left untouched for eons. As he carefully scanned the room, Tails found one peculiar document that had seemingly fallen on the ground. He unfolded it gently and began to read.\n“What’s this? ‘Cloning Experiment Log’?” Tails wondered aloud, his curiosity burning even brighter. As he continued to peruse the document, a terrible truth began to dawn on him. All of the characters transported to this planetoid, himself included, were not the originals, but mere clones of these warriors. The original ones were still living their lives in their respective universes, unaware of the existence of their duplicates on this colossal celestial body.\nTails reeled from this revelation, his world instantly turning upside down. He knew that his friends, as well as their newfound allies, would need to know the truth. He raced back to the makeshift village where Sonic, Knuckles, and the other characters had set up their base of operations.\n“Sonic! Knuckles! Guys, I’ve got something important to tell you!” Tails shouted, waving the document as he entered their shared living space.\n“What’s up, Tails? You seem more freaked out than usual,” Sonic said, looking up from a map he had been studying closely with Knuckles.\n“It’s… it’s this document I found,” Tails panted, catching his breath. “You guys… we’re clones. All of us.”\nThe room went silent as they all processed the shocking news. Denial was the first reaction, as Knuckles scoffed, “Clones? Seriously, Tails? That’s ridiculous.”\n“No, it’s true!” Tails insisted. “I found the logs of the experiments that created us, and according to this, each of us was created here on the planetoid, while our originals live out their lives in our home universes!”\nThe grim revelation sent shockwaves through every character that eventually find out about this revelation. Some characters were devastated, while others questioned their own existence and purpose.\n\nChapter 2\n“What do you mean we’re clones?” Katsuki Bakugo roared, his voice nearly shaking the makeshift wooden walls around him. The explosive and short-tempered hero from the My Hero Academia universe was not known for his patience, and the frustration that filled the room was palpable.\nAcross from him, Jiren the Grey – an extraterrestrial who had come from the Dragon Ball Super universe with immense power – narrowed his eyes, studying the document that Tails had brought back. He remained calm and composed, despite the escalating tension that threatened to boil over. “It would seem that we have all been duped,” Jiren said somberly.\nWord had spread quickly among the inhabitants of that sumerian village, and the news of their true identities had attracted everyone’s attention. Some heroes and villains alike had gathered in the small common area to discuss the sudden revelation, but most of them could not believe what they had just been told.\n“I refuse to believe it!” Bakugo bellowed, his fists igniting with fury. “I have my own dreams and goals in life! I want to become the number one hero in my universe! And now you’re telling me that I’m just a carbon copy?”\n“I understand your anger, Bakugo,” Jiren replied, his composure remaining unbroken. “But instead of cursing the darkness, we should light a candle.”\n“What are you babbling about, alien?” Bakugo snorted, glaring at Jiren as if he was a mortal enemy.\nJiren sighed and looked around at the diverse crowd that had gathered. “I’m simply saying that we should use this information to our advantage. Perhaps we can find a way to return to our respective universes and confront our original selves.”\n“But why?” asked Sonic, who had previously been leaning against a wall with folded arms. “If this is true, then what’s the point of going back? We can’t just replace our originals, and even if we could, what would that make us? Are we just destined to be second-best for the rest of our existence?”\n“Who said anything about replacing them, Sonic?” Jiren countered. “But we have the right to know who we are, where we came from, and why we were created.”\n“We could also try to find a way to contact our original selves,” Tails suggested hesitantly, his twin tails twitching nervously. “Maybe there’s something they can do to help us. I know it’s a long shot, but it’s worth a try, right?”\nThe room went silent for a moment, and then Knuckles, who had been standing silently in a corner, spoke up. “You know what? Tails is right. Sure, we’re clones, but we exist. And since we’re here, we might as well make the best of it.”\nBakugo scowled but didn’t say anything. Knuckles continued, “Jiren’s got a point, too. We have to at least try to find out more about our origins and purpose. So, what do you say, everybody? ‘单项合规性’：针对列表中的每一项，分别判断其是否符合约束条件（如位置、费用）。若某一项不合规，应在该维度扣分，但不能直接判定整个回答在‘位置’或‘相关性’维度上得分为0。只有当任务明确限定‘只允许列出...且不能有其他’时，才采用一票否决制。避免使用模糊的‘回答中列出的地点是否都位于...'作为唯一标准，而应细化为‘回答中提供的每个地点是否均满足...'，并据此计算得分比例或分项扣分。", "必须将‘文档排版的视觉丰富度与层次感’作为独立的高权重核验维度，严格区分‘基础结构合规’与‘符合文体美学预期的专业呈现’，避免将简单的列表或标题视为满足所有排版要求。", "application_guide": "retrieval_source": "semantic", "historical_reward": null, "evidence_tasks": 0}, {"experience_id": "7", "trigger": "当任务要求生成内容中包含特定角色的心理状态、情感态度或性格特质（如‘不感到抱歉’、‘坚决’、‘愤怒’等），且这些状态无法通过简单的关键词匹配直接验证时", "principle": "必须将‘心理状态的显性陈述与隐性行为的一致性’作为独立核验维度，严禁仅凭是否存在礼貌用语或语气强弱来单一判定态度是否符合，需同时验证模型是否通过直接心理描写或具体行为逻辑准确呈现了目标状态。", "application_guide": "Generator 在设计 Rubric 时，针对态度类要求应设立两条互补准则：1. ‘显性确认’：检查故事中是否有角色直接陈述其心理状态（如‘我不感到抱歉’、‘我坚持要答案’）；2. ‘行为逻辑一致性’：检查角色的对话和行为是否排除了与该态度相悖的要素（如‘不感到抱歉’则不应出现‘希望你不介意’等道歉缓冲语，‘坚决’则不应表现出犹豫或退缩）。只有当两者结合评估，确认角色既表达了该态度且在行为上未表现出相反倾向时，才判定为达标。避免仅因使用了礼貌词汇就判定为‘感到抱歉’，也避免仅因没有直接说‘我不抱歉’而忽略其坚定的行为表现。", "retrieval_source": "semantic", "historical_reward": null, "evidence_tasks": 0}, {"experience_id": "8", "trigger": "当任务要求生成内容长度严格限定为'only N characters/words'时", "principle": "必须将'only N'的语义明确定义为'精确等于N'还是'不超过N'，避免默认假设为上限导致与用户真实意图（特别是需要精确长度时）不符，或因默认假设为精确值导致对轻微超出的过度惩罚。", "application_guide": "Generator在设计评分标准时，若遇到'only N'的长度约束，不应直接使用模糊的'不超过'或'等于'，而应结合上下文判断：若上下文暗示是最大容量（如'limit', 'max'），则设为'不超过'；若上下文强调精确规格（如'exact length', 'precise count'），则设为'精确等于'。若上下文不明，应在标准中明确写出'精确等于N'或'不超过N'，并保留注释说明选择依据，防止因术语歧义导致评分失效。", "retrieval_source": "semantic", "historical_reward": null, "evidence_tasks": 0}, {"experience_id": "9", "trigger": "当任务要求对明显违背常识或生物学/物理定律的前提进行数量估算或可行性推演（如动物做人类工作、永动机等），且用户语气包含幽默、好奇或‘脑洞’特征时", "principle": "在此类‘荒谬假设’场景中，提供具体的虚构数值是回应问题的核心要素，不应被视为‘事实错误’或‘误导性信息’；评分标准必须将‘基于假设的创造性推演’与‘事实陈述错误’解耦，禁止设立‘禁止提供具体数值’的负面约束。", "application_guide": "Generator在设计评分标准时，若检测到任务前提为明显荒谬且用户寻求估算/推演：1. 必须移除任何禁止提供具体数值的准则；2. 将‘是否构建了一个自洽的虚构逻辑链条并给出估算结果’设为正向得分点；3. 仅当回答完全拒绝参与推演（如仅说‘不可能’而无后续互动）时才扣分；4. 除非用户明确要求‘解释为什么不行’，否则不应将‘指出生物学不可能性’作为最高权重的否决项，而应将其作为背景说明处理。", "retrieval_source": "semantic", "historical_reward": null, "必须设立独立的‘文档结构完整性’核验准则，专门验证交付物是否具备该类型文档的标准骨架（如目标陈述、核心策略、实施步骤、结论等），将其作为判定是否满足任务核心意图的首要条件，而非仅关注内容细节的丰富度。", "application_guide": "Generator 0.3-0.4），明确提问‘是否包含了[类型]所需的所有基本结构模块？’。只有当模型输出了符合该结构的完整文档骨架时，才视为满足基础要求；若模型仅输出碎片化的要点列表而无整体框架，无论内容多么准确，都应在此维度判为不合格，从而有效区分‘信息罗列’与‘规划生成’。", "retrieval_source": "semantic", "historical_reward": null,
